# Fact-check: the book plan

This log checks the factual claims in [build-book.md](../build-book.md), ADRs [0052](../../decisions/0052-book-in-apps-on-cloudflare.md), [0053](../../decisions/0053-book-stack-and-deploy.md) and [0054](../../decisions/0054-reuse-garment-textbook-code.md), todos T1–T48 and U18–U21, and the code and docs that T1–T4 added. Claims were checked on 2026-11-19 against four kinds of source. Repo claims were read at `main` `eddc212` in the first passes and at `ea70a57` from the sixth pass on. Package claims come from `npm view <package> time`, `engines`, `peerDependencies` and the packed `.d.ts`. Cloudflare claims come from developers.cloudflare.com, read through Cloudflare's documentation search. Garment textbook claims come from `tajistudios/garment-engineering-textbook` at commit `bca44b0`, read with `gh api`. Design choices that are our own proposals are not facts and have no row. Rows follow the numbers the plan cites, so some ranges hold related claims in order.

**Row ids.** A row with a plain number, such as 347, comes from the log's old global sequence and keeps that number. A row added since 2026-11-19 takes `<todo id>-<n>`, such as `T31-2`, or `<branch>-<n>` for work without a todo, such as `docs/fix-links-1`, so parallel pull requests never take the same id. A branch id is the branch name, `/` kept, then a hyphen and the count, so `deps/astro-5.2` gives `deps/astro-5.2-1`. The name starts with a lower-case letter, holds letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-` and `/`, and ends in a letter, digit or `_`; the count is the digits after the last hyphen. A citation such as "row 347" or "row T31-2" names a row in either form. Never renumber a row. Don't count the totals by hand either: `pnpm fact-check-logs --write` rewrites the totals line ([AGENTS.md](../../../AGENTS.md#every-document-is-drafted-fact-checked-and-reviewed-before-it-merges)).

### T6's second review round

The medium review of PR #71 found that the scroll hint showed when nothing scrolled, so row 337's threshold was wrong. These rows correct it and check the claims of the fix, measured in headless Chrome 157 on a fixture build with the chapter page in an iframe of the stated width.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 342 | Row 337, corrected: a 37.5rem threshold assumed 2.5rem of stage padding, but the padding is `clamp(1rem, 4vw, 2.5rem)`, so from about 617 to 649 px the hint showed with no overflow, and it would show under a narrower figure that fits | BK11, `components.css` | ✏️ | `components.css` `.figure__stage` padding; the review measured 640 px: panel 591, `scrollWidth` equal to `clientWidth`, hint shown. **Now:** the hint follows the stage's own overflow (row 343), and the 37.5rem rule is gone |
| 343 | A scroll timeline is inactive while its container has nothing to scroll, so an animation on it has no effect and the hint keeps its hidden base style | `components.css`, README, BK11 | ✅ | [CSS Scroll-driven Animations](https://www.w3.org/TR/scroll-animations-1/), the `ScrollTimeline` interface: "if there is no scrollable overflow, then the ScrollTimeline is inactive"; checked in Chrome by row 344 |
| 344 | At 360, 480, 640 and 1280 px the hint shows exactly when a stage's `scrollWidth` exceeds its `clientWidth`, for both 520-wide samples and a 300-wide copy | BK11, commit | ✅ | Ours. Stage `clientWidth`/`scrollWidth`, hint shown: 360 px, 326/552 shown for the 520-wide figures and 326/332 shown for the 300-wide copy; 480 px, 440/558 shown and 440/440 hidden; 640 px, 591/591 hidden for all; 1280 px, 606/606 hidden for all |
| 345 | The build's CSS minifier folds `animation` and `animation-timeline` in one rule into an `animation` shorthand that holds the timeline, which Chrome rejects, so the timeline sits in a later rule | `components.css` | ✅ | Ours: the built CSS read `animation:linear both figure-scroll-hint --figure-scroll`, and the hint never showed until the timeline moved to its own rule. MDN's [`animation-timeline`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Properties/animation-timeline) page: "The animation-timeline property is included in the animation shorthand as a reset-only value" (a secondary source) |
| 346 | Every scrolling stage keeps `tabindex="0"`, because CSS cannot make an element focusable only while it overflows; a figure that fits is then a named tab stop that scrolls nothing | BK11, README | ✅ | Ours: focusability comes from the `tabindex` attribute, which no CSS property sets; scoping it needs a script, which BK11 rules out |

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 347 | Row 345, corrected: the primary source does not make `animation-timeline` reset-only in the `animation` shorthand; the shorthand's grammar includes `<single-animation-timeline>`. Chrome 157 still rejects the shorthand the minifier wrote, so the timeline stays in its own rule | Row 345, `components.css` | ✏️ | [CSS Animations Level 2, §4.12, the `animation` shorthand](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-animations-2/#animation-shorthand), Editor's Draft of 18 August 2026, and §4.10 of the Working Draft of 2 June 2023: both grammars end in `\|\| <single-animation-timeline>`. MDN's "reset-only value" does not match them. The shorthand still resets the timeline when it omits it, as it does every sub-property; the comment now says the grammar allows the minifier's shorthand and Chrome rejects it |

### T5's second review round

The medium-effort review found that one Cloudflare transcript changed order between runs on CI, that the id and temporary-directory rules could rewrite what an example scripts, and that a check could be forged from example output. Rows 401–404 correct rows 383, 394 and 397, and row 407, from the low-effort review, corrects row 405.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 401 | Row 394, corrected: workerd's console lines reach the runner in the order the work happens, which for `09-triage-agent.ts` raced: the model's answer in the turn an approval continues came before or after the next two checks. The example now waits for that answer before those checks, so its events come in one order | `verify/cloudflare-agents/README.md`, `examples/09-triage-agent.ts` | ✏️ | CI run 44564644334 failed results-drift on `09-triage-agent.json`: event 8 was the answer in the committed file and a check on CI. With the wait, seven runs here, three of them beside other test runs at a load average of about 45, wrote identical transcripts with every example passing |
| 402 | Row 397, corrected: the two-run check held on this machine but missed that race; four runs of each of the five other harnesses and seven of Cloudflare's now leave every transcript byte-identical | The todo's Outcome | ✏️ | `diff -r` after each run on 2026-11-19 printed nothing; the 47 files changed only where rows 403 and 404 say |
| 403 | Row 383, corrected: the writer keeps an id as written exactly when the mock's script chose it, which each recorder reports as the response's `scripted` list, and replaces every other id, whatever its length | `scripts/transcripts/write.mjs`, the harness libraries | ✏️ | Our choice, after the review. The shape rule had turned the OpenAI example's scripted `call_handoff` into `<id-1>`; it now stays. These rows check the claims in `wrangler.jsonc`, the guard, the artifact check, `book-deploy.yml`, the README, AGENTS.md and BK15's note. ### T27's first review round

The high-effort review found that one job held both keys where any example could read them, that the environment's branch rule and bypass were weaker than the docs said, and that a page claimed a comparison its listings never had. Rows 528–534 record the checks behind the fixes; rows 531–533 correct earlier claims.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 528 | Reading an environment, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/environments/{environment_name}`, needs the Actions repository permission at read, so each provider's job asks for `actions: read` | `live-run.yml` | ✅ | [GitHub, permissions required for fine-grained personal access tokens](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/authentication/permissions-required-for-fine-grained-personal-access-tokens), under "Repository permissions for 'Actions'": "`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/environments/{environment_name}` \| read" |
| 529 | Listing branches, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches`, needs Contents at read; as of 2026-11-19 `branches?protected=true` lists only `main`, and the repository has no rulesets | `live-run.yml`, `scripts/ci/live-run.mjs` (`protected`) | ✅ | The same table, under "Contents": "`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches` \| read"; `gh api` on 2026-11-19 returned `["main"]` and `[]` for `/rulesets` |
| 530 | As of 2026-11-19, after the owner's change, `live-run` lets no administrator bypass its rules, and still requires `laudebugs` and allows protected branches only | The todo's Outcome, BK18, the manual's README | ✅ | `gh api repos/laudebugs/buildanagent/environments/live-run` on 2026-11-19: `can_admins_bypass: false`, `deployment_branch_policy: {protected_branches: true, custom_branch_policies: false}` |
| 531 | The todo's Outcome and the manual's README, corrected: deleting `LIVE_API_KEY` from an example's environment did not hide the key, because `/proc/<pid>/environ` keeps the environment a program started with, and run.mjs held both keys in its own. The keys now travel only in private files that each reader deletes | `scripts/live/live.ts`, `scripts/live/run.mjs`, the manual's README, the todo's Outcome | ✏️ | [proc_pid_environ(5)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man5/proc_pid_environ.5.html): "This file contains the initial environment that was set when the currently executing program was started via execve(2)", and later changes through putenv(3) or environ(7) are not reflected |
| 532 | The manual's README, corrected: "Protected branches only" admits every branch with protection rules, not `main` alone, so each provider's job fails when any other branch is protected | The manual's README | ✏️ | Row 514's source: "Only branches with branch protection rules enabled can deploy to the environment" |

| 534 | An Anthropic workspace can have a monthly spend limit, and an OpenAI project a hard spend limit that stops its API traffic once reached | The manual's README, BK18 | ✅ | [Claude workspaces](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/workspaces), "Spend limits: Cap monthly spending for a workspace"; [OpenAI production best practices](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/production-best-practices): "Hard spend limits stop affected API traffic when tracked spend reaches the limit", and "set custom rate and spend limits per project", both read 2026-11-19 |

## T29: the retries, caching and budget example

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 565 | `generateText` retries a failed model call, 2 times by default, and `maxRetries: 0` turns retries off | T29, `examples/12-retries-caching-budget.ts` header | ✅ | `ai@7.0.162` `dist/index.d.ts` (`@param maxRetries`: "Maximum number of retries. Set to 0 to disable retries. Default: 2.") and `prepareRetries` in `dist/index.js` |
| 566 | An error is retried only when it is an `APICallError` whose `isRetryable` is `true` (or a gateway error that says so); a non-retryable error on the first attempt is thrown as it is, and running out of retries throws a `RetryError` with reason `maxRetriesExceeded` | T29, header and checks | ✅ | `dist/index.js` `retryWithExponentialBackoffRespectingRetryHeaders` (`shouldRetry`) and `@ai-sdk/provider-utils@5.0.74` `retryWithExponentialBackoffInternal` (`tryNumber === 1` rethrows; `tryNumber > maxRetries` gives `maxRetriesExceeded`); the example's checks run all three paths |
| 567 | The SDK waits 2 seconds before the first retry unless the error carries a `retry-after-ms` or `retry-after` header of 0 to 60 seconds, which it uses instead; the example's `retry-after-ms: "0"` therefore retries at once | T29, header and `retry` listing | ✅ | `initialDelayInMs = 2e3` and `getRetryDelayInMs` in `dist/index.js` (accepts a header value from 0 up to under 60,000 ms) |
| 568 | `wrapLanguageModel` takes a middleware whose `wrapGenerate` receives `doGenerate` and `params`, so a throw before `doGenerate()` keeps the call from the model | T29, header and `budget` listing | ✅ | `LanguageModelV4Middleware.wrapGenerate` in `@ai-sdk/provider@4.0.35` `dist/index.d.ts` and `wrapLanguageModel` in `ai`'s `dist/index.d.ts`; the example's last check counts the mock's calls |
| 569 | `generateText`'s result reports cache-read and cache-write input tokens as `usage.inputTokenDetails.cacheReadTokens` and `cacheWriteTokens` | T29, `cache` listing | ✅ | `LanguageModelUsage` in `ai`'s `dist/index.d.ts` |
| 570 | Claude Sonnet 5.5 costs $2 per million uncached input tokens, $0.10 per million cache-read tokens, $2.50 per million cache-write tokens (5-minute cache) and $10 per million output tokens, as of 2026-11-19 | T29, header and `prices` listing | ✅ | `packages/gateway/src/catalog.json`, entry `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5`, `asOf` 2026-11-19, sourced from the pricing page it names; the example takes the same rates from `examples/lib/rates.ts` (`SONNET_5_5`: 2,000, 100, 2,500 and 10,000 nano-dollars per token), which copies that page |
| 571 | Our choices, not the SDK's: the example estimates tokens as characters divided by four, projects a call at its worst case (no cache hit, a full-length answer), sets a $0.05 budget, and counts the budget as money already spent plus the projection | T29, `prices` and `budget` listings | ✏️ | The mock reports the same estimate, so projection and usage agree; the checks confirm that the three calls spend under half the budget and that a call whose projection passes it is refused Corrected by row 574: the first draft projected a cache miss at the input rate with a fixed output cap, and only the projection alone was tested |
| 572 | No second wire protocol is shown, because a mock HTTP server behind a provider's base URL replaces the transport below the model interface, where the recorder and this mock sit | T29, header | ✏️ | Wrong, corrected by row 573: a second protocol can be shown offline, and the recorder still stores the example's `check()` events (the transcript was not empty) |
| 573 | `createAnthropic` takes `baseURL` and `fetch`, so a function that answers in Anthropic's Messages format runs the same retry, cache and budget calls over a second wire protocol with no network; the provider turns an HTTP 529 into an `APICallError` that is retryable because its status is 408, 409, 429 or 500 and above, and a `cacheControl` provider option on the system message marks the cached prefix | T29, header and `wire` listing | ✅ | `@ai-sdk/anthropic@4.0.96` `dist/index.d.ts` (`baseURL` line 1805, `fetch` line 1827, `cacheControl` line 609); `@ai-sdk/provider@4.0.35` `APICallError` default `isRetryable` (`dist/index.js` line 70). The example sends 529 then 200 and counts three requests for two calls, and reads `cache_read_input_tokens` back as `cacheReadTokens` |
| 574 | Our choices, replacing row 571: the budget refuses a call when money already spent plus the call's projection passes $0.03; the projection prices the system-message prefix as a cache write ($2.50 per million), the rest of the prompt as uncached input and the answer at the call's own `maxOutputTokens` (500 when unset); tokens are estimated as characters divided by four | T29, `prices` and `budget` listings | ✏️ | Our design, tested: a call whose projection ($0.0249) fits alone is refused because $0.0114 is spent; a long prefix with a 20-token limit projects $0.01015, which only the cache-write rate and the call's own limit produce; the cached call costs under a fifth of the same call uncached. Rates as in row 570 Corrected by row 575: the cache-write rate was not tested, because the expected value used the same rates as the code |
| 575 | Our choices, replacing row 574's test: the projection of a call with a 3,973-token prefix, an 8-token question and a 20-token answer is (3,973 × $2.50 + 8 × $2 + 20 × $10) / 1,000,000 = $0.0101485, and the same call at the input rate would be $0.0081620; the Messages-format budget refusal, like the scripted one, is a call that fits alone ($0.0249) and is refused because $0.0108 is spent; the Messages-format exchange leaves no request or answer events in the transcript | T29, `budget` listing, header and README | ✅ | The example asserts the literal 3,973 × 2,500 + 8 × 2,000 + 20 × 10,000 = 10,148,500 nano-dollars exactly, which fails if `SONNET_5_5.cacheWrite` is changed to 2,000; rates as in row 570. `examples/lib/transcript.ts` attaches the recorder to `MockLanguageModelV4`, and `createAnthropic` builds its own model, so the transcript holds that exchange's `check()` events only |

## The first preview on the custom domain

Book-deploy run 44588564990, for pull request #89 on 2026-11-19 at 18:31 UTC, made the first preview on `manual.buildanagent.fyi`. The comment job ran and posted nothing, because the commit's URL failed the check. Rows 576–579 record what that run showed. Wrangler's lines come from the run's log; the headers were read from the two preview URLs and from production on the same day, before this change. Row 509, the comment's author, stayed ⚠️ until a comment posted; PR #94's comment settled it, in row docs/book-owner-decisions-1.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 576 | Rows 496 and 504, corrected: on the custom domain, a deployment's own URL is `https://<deployment id>.manual.buildanagent.fyi` with an id of 8 lowercase hex digits, not `<deployment-id>-<preview-name>.<hostname>`. The record step and the comment script now accept exactly `^https://[0-9a-f]{8}\.manual\.buildanagent\.fyi$` for it, and still refuse it when it equals the alias | workflow's URL check, the comment script, README, BK15 | ✏️ | Run 44588564990's log: Wrangler 4.160.1 printed "Preview URL: https://pr-89.manual.buildanagent.fyi" and "Unique Deployment URL: https://40f313de.manual.buildanagent.fyi". Cloudflare's [Previews](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/previews/) page gives the other form. The 8-hex-digit rule rests on that one id; a different form still costs one warning and no comment, never a wrong URL |
| 577 | Row 502, settled: the preview token, Workers Editor on the book's Worker with no zone permission, is enough to create a preview on the custom domain | README | ✅ | Run 44588564990 deployed `pr-89` with that token, and both of its URLs answered HTTP 200 with a valid certificate |

| docs/book-owner-decisions-1 | Row 509, settled: the comments the preview job makes with `GITHUB_TOKEN` are authored by `github-actions[bot]`, a `Bot` account, and the job edits its one comment instead of posting another | workflow, README | ✅ | `GET /repos/laudebugs/agent-speak/issues/94/comments`, read on 2026-11-19: comment 7018962536 on PR #94 has user `github-actions[bot]`, type `Bot`. Its edit history (GraphQL `userContentEdits`) shows one comment, first posted at 18:56:37 UTC for commit d903e84, edited at 19:08:35 for a1039c8 to list "Pull request preview, which follows its newest commit: https://pr-94.manual.buildanagent.fyi" and "This commit only: https://4779651c.manual.buildanagent.fyi", then edited to "Book preview deleted" when the pull request closed |

| T49-54 | The stub's OpenAI stream adds a message empty, then each content part empty, sends the text in deltas, and holds the whole item only in `output_item.done`, as the real API's stream does | `live-stub.mjs` | ⚠️ | The stub's shape is in `scripts/ci/live-stub.mjs`, and the live test against it passes. That the real API streams in this order is the manager session's review of the PR (finding 7), which names `output_item.added`, `content_part.added`, the deltas and `output_item.done`; it was not checked here against OpenAI's streaming reference, and no run called the API |

## Issue #174: a reader's place and the resume link

Claims in BK23 and the README's "A reader's place stays in their own browser", checked on 2026-11-22 against MDN, the HTML standard and a Chromium run of the built book. The design choices in BK23 are our own and have no row.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fix/174-reading-position-1 | localStorage keeps a separate store for each origin, and its data stays across browser sessions with no expiry | BK23, README | ✅ | [MDN, `Window.localStorage`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/localStorage): a `Storage` object "for the Document's origin; the stored data is saved across browser sessions" and "`localStorage` data has no expiration time"; [Web Storage API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_Storage_API): "`localStorage` is partitioned by origin only" |
| fix/174-reading-position-2 | With storage blocked, reading `localStorage` throws, and a full store makes `setItem` throw | BK23, README, `reading.js` | ✅ | [MDN, `Window.localStorage`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/localStorage): `SecurityError` when "the user has configured the browsers to prevent the page from persisting data", and blocking cookies probably counts; [MDN, `Storage.setItem()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage/setItem): `QuotaExceededError` when storage runs out of quota. `test/reading.test.ts` and the Chromium run simulate both by throwing from the getter and from each call |
| fix/174-reading-position-3 | Storage like localStorage never sends its data to the server, unlike a cookie, which goes with every request | BK23 | ✅ | [MDN, Using HTTP cookies](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/Cookies): "Cookies are sent with every request" and the Web Storage API and IndexedDB "never send data to the server". `reading.js` names no network API (`test/reading.test.ts`), and the Chromium run saw no request outside the local server |
| fix/174-reading-position-4 | A reader removes the stored places by clearing the site's data in the browser's settings | BK23, README | ✅ | [MDN, Storage quotas and eviction criteria](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria): an origin's best-effort data, the default, persists until "the user … choose[s] to delete the data via their browser's settings"; the page states this for an origin's data in general, which includes Web Storage |
| fix/174-reading-position-5 | A module script runs once the page is parsed | README | ✅ | [MDN, `<script>`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/script): module scripts "defer by default"; `BaseLayout.astro` loads `reading.js` with `type="module"` |
| fix/174-reading-position-6 | A browser that finds no element for a link's fragment leaves the page where it opened, so a lost heading id opens the chapter at its top | BK23, README, `reading.js` | ✅ | [HTML, scroll to the fragment](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsing-the-web.html#scroll-to-the-fragment-identifier): when the indicated part is null, the algorithm only sets the target element to null and does not scroll. The Chromium run opened `/tools/#a-removed-section` at `scrollY` 0 |
| fix/174-reading-position-7 | A heading id escaped with `encodeURIComponent` in the link still finds its heading | `reading.js` | ✅ | [HTML, select the indicated part](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsing-the-web.html#select-the-indicated-part): the fragment is percent-decoded and UTF-8 decoded before the second id lookup. The Chromium run landed the followed link's heading at the top of the window |
| fix/174-reading-position-8 | Browsers don't fire the page-hide event every time, so a place taken during the last pause can be lost | README | ✅ | [MDN, `pagehide`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/pagehide_event): "this event is not reliably fired by browsers, especially on mobile" |
| fix/174-reading-position-9 | The site header's row wraps on a narrow screen, and the Contents list does not move when the link appears | BK23, README | ✅ | The Chromium run at 390 px: the header holds two rows, and the contents' top sat at 379.27 px both before any place was stored and with the link shown; the only layout shift on the page, a font swap in the contents list, happens with or without the link |
| fix/174-reading-position-10 | The content security policy allows a same-origin script by path | README | ✅ | `public/_headers`: `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`; the Chromium run served that policy and logged no violation |
| fix/174-reading-position-11 | A page restored from the back/forward cache runs no script again, and its `pageshow` event says so with `persisted` | README, `reading.js` | ✅ | [MDN, bfcache](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/bfcache): the snapshot holds "the entire page in memory, including the JavaScript heap; in-progress code is paused … and resumed when they return"; [MDN, `PageTransitionEvent.persisted`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/PageTransitionEvent/persisted): it "indicates if a webpage is loading from a cache", read on `pageshow`. The Chromium run's Back navigation restored the Contents page with `persisted` true and the link updated |

## Issue #172: glossary terms link once per paragraph

Claims in BK7's #172 result, the README's "Glossary terms link once per paragraph" and the comments in `remark-terms.ts` and `scripts/glossary.ts`, checked on 2026-11-22 against WCAG, MDN, two builds of the book (before and after the change) and a Chromium run. Linking once per paragraph is the owner's decision and has no row.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-1 | Until this change, only the component gallery showed a glossary card; a chapter's term links had none | BK7 | ✅ | The build from the branch's base (`54ac610`): chapter pages rendered `<a class="term" data-def=…>`, no stylesheet or script selected `.term` or `data-def`, and `GlossaryTerm.astro`, the only markup with `.glossary-term__card`, appeared only on `/gallery/` |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-2 | WCAG 1.4.13 asks that content shown on hover or focus can be dismissed without moving hover or focus, that the pointer can move onto it without it disappearing, and that it stays until the trigger is removed or the user dismisses it | README, `glossary.ts` | ✅ | [Understanding SC 1.4.13](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/content-on-hover-or-focus.html): Dismissible, "without moving pointer hover or keyboard focus"; Hoverable, "the pointer can be moved over the additional content without the additional content disappearing"; Persistent, "remains visible until the hover or focus trigger is removed, the user dismisses it" |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-3 | The content security policy blocks style attributes in markup but not styles a script sets through the element's `style` object | `glossary.ts` | ✅ | [MDN, CSP `style-src`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/style-src): inline style attributes "are also blocked", while "styles properties that are set directly on the element's `style` property will not be blocked". The Chromium run served `public/_headers`' policy, logged no violation, and found every card placed inside the viewport |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-4 | Screen readers skip the card, because `aria-hidden="true"` removes it from the accessibility tree, and hear the definition through `aria-describedby` | README, `remark-terms.ts` | ✅ | [MDN, `aria-hidden`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Accessibility/ARIA/Reference/Attributes/aria-hidden): it "removes that element and all of its children from the accessibility tree". The Chromium run found each link's `aria-describedby` target (`termdef-tool`) once on `/tools/` |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-5 | Once per paragraph, "session" links 42 times in Chapter 5 and "tool" 43 times in Chapter 3 | BK7, README | ✅ | Counted in the built `memory-and-sessions/index.html` (Chapter 5) and `tools/index.html` (Chapter 3) from this branch: 42 `href="/glossary/#session"` and 43 `href="/glossary/#tool"` links |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-6 | A card never makes a 390 px screen scroll sideways, a scrolling table does not clip it, and the pointer can move from the term onto the card while it stays open | README | ✅ | The Chromium run of the built book at 390 px: hovering each of `/tools/`'s 49 visible terms left `scrollWidth` equal to `clientWidth` and every card inside the viewport; a table cell's card on `/memory-and-sessions/` was the topmost element at its own top edge, above the table; moving the pointer from a term up through the gap into its card kept the card visible at 390 px and 1280 px |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-7 | Firefox's reader view drops `aria-hidden` elements, because its Readability library removes them | README | ✅ | [`mozilla/readability`, `Readability.js`](https://github.com/mozilla/readability/blob/main/Readability.js), `main` read 2026-11-22: `_isProbablyVisible` returns false for a node whose `aria-hidden` is `"true"` (unless its class names `fallback-image`), and `_grabArticle` removes every node that fails it ("Removing hidden node"). The repository's README calls it "A standalone version of the readability library used for Firefox Reader View"; Firefox's own source was not read |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-8 | In Chapter 3 the cards and their markup add 24.7 KB to the page, or 1.2 KB once compressed, as of November 2026 | README, BK7 | ✅ | `tools/index.html` from the branch's base build (`54ac610`) was 99,511 bytes, 22,502 gzipped; from this branch 124,169 bytes, 23,679 gzipped: 24,658 and 1,177 bytes more. The difference includes each link's wrapper span and class change, not only the card text |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-9 | The card markup is valid HTML in paragraphs, list items, table cells and captions, and adds no validator error | Review of PR #240 | ✅ | `html-validate` 9.4.0 with `html-validate:standard`, run from outside the repo on every built chapter and the glossary page, before and after: the same messages both times (one `element-permitted-order` per chapter page and `long-title` on two), none in a term's markup. A `<span>` is phrasing content, which `<p>`, `<li>`, `<td>` and `<figcaption>` all accept, and the card is a sibling of the link, not inside it |
| fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-10 | A term link's accessible name is the term alone, its description is the definition, and the page has one tab stop per visible term link and none inside a card | Review of PR #240 | ✅ | Chromium's accessibility tree on `/tools/`: term links named "tool" and "tools", each described by the definition, and no other accessible node holds the card text; tabbing through the page stopped on 49 term links, the 49 visible ones, and never inside a card, which holds no link or `tabindex` |

## Issue #173: a reader's notes and highlights

Claims in BK24, the README's "A reader's notes stay in their own browser", its token table row and the comments in `public/scripts/notes.js` and `components.css`, checked on 2026-11-22 against MDN, the HTML and CSP specifications, MDN's browser-compat-data at commit `1edef63` and a Chromium run of the built book. The Chromium run used Chrome for Testing 153.0.8010.12 through Playwright 1.63.0, outside the repository, against `dist/` served with the production `Content-Security-Policy` from `public/_headers`. The design choices in BK24 are our own and have no row.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-1 | The CSS Custom Highlight API draws a highlight from `Range` objects without changing the page's elements: a `Highlight` takes ranges, `CSS.highlights` registers it by name and `::highlight(<name>)` styles it | BK24, README, `notes.js` | ✅ | [MDN, CSS Custom Highlight API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/CSS_Custom_Highlight_API): "create text ranges and highlight them without affecting the DOM structure", "`new Highlight(range1, range2)`", "register them by using the `HighlightRegistry` available as `CSS.highlights`" and "style the registered highlights … by using the `::highlight()` pseudo-element". The Chromium run found no added element in the article after highlighting |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-2 | As of November 2026 the API works in Chrome from version 105, Safari from 17.2 and Firefox from 140 | BK24, README | ✅ | [browser-compat-data `api/Highlight.json`](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/1edef63031c8bbc2844b3f48e092433eecc4842e/api/Highlight.json): `version_added` 105 (Chrome), 140 (Firefox), 17.2 (Safari); `css/selectors/highlight.json` gives the same for Chrome and Safari |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-3 | Firefox before version 146 draws a highlight's background but not its underline | BK24, README | ✅ | [browser-compat-data `css/selectors/highlight.json`](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/1edef63031c8bbc2844b3f48e092433eecc4842e/css/selectors/highlight.json): Firefox 140 to 148 is a partial implementation, "Before Firefox 146, cannot be used with `text-decoration`" (bug 1845446). Not run in Firefox |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-4 | `::highlight()` accepts `color`, `background-color` and the `text-decoration` properties, the only ones the highlight rules set | `components.css` | ✅ | [MDN, `::highlight()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/::highlight): "Only certain CSS properties can be used with `::highlight()`: `color`, `background-color`, `text-decoration` and its associated properties, `text-shadow`" and the `-webkit-text-*` properties. The rules set `background-color`, `color`, `text-decoration-line`, `-color`, `-thickness` and `-style` |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-5 | A highlight shows the pale `--color-highlight` background and an underline in `--color-accent` in both themes | README, `components.css` | ✅ | The Chromium run's screenshots, sampled pixel by pixel: the light theme drew `#f5df9e` with an underline of `#b04e29`, the dark theme `#4f3b1c` with `#e8a27c`, the values `tokens.css` ships, so the custom properties resolve inside `::highlight()` |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-6 | Text on the highlight keeps 4.5:1 (12.3:1 light, 8.94:1 dark), the accent underline reaches 3:1 on it (4.02:1 light, 5.01:1 dark), and the highlight stays below 3:1 on the page (1.2:1 light, 1.52:1 dark) | README, `book-tokens.json` | ✅ | `test/contrast.test.ts` recomputes each recorded pair from the colours `tokens.css` ships with the WCAG formula, and its highlight test checks the rule uses those three tokens |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-7 | localStorage is kept per origin and never sent to the server, unlike a cookie | BK24, README | ✅ | [MDN, Using HTTP cookies](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/Cookies): the Web Storage API and IndexedDB "never send data to the server"; [MDN, Web Storage API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_Storage_API): "`localStorage` is partitioned by origin only". `test/notes.test.ts` fails on any network API in the script, and the Chromium run saw no request outside the local server |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-8 | A browser stores up to about 5 MiB of localStorage per origin and throws `QuotaExceededError` past it, which the script catches and reports | README, `notes.js` | ✅ | [MDN, Storage quotas and eviction criteria](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria): "Browsers can store up to 5 MiB of local storage … per origin" and "browsers throw a `QuotaExceededError` exception". The same page also gives 10 MiB for Web Storage in all, so the README names no figure. `test/notes.test.ts` makes every write throw and checks the message |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-9 | Clearing the site's data in the browser's settings deletes the notes | BK24, README | ✅ | [MDN, Storage quotas and eviction criteria](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria): best-effort data persists until "the user … choose[s] to delete the data via their browser's settings" |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-10 | A modal `<dialog>` closes on Escape and makes the rest of the page inert | README | ✅ | [MDN, `<dialog>`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/dialog): "a dialog invoked by the `showModal()` method can be dismissed by pressing the Esc key" and modal dialogs make "the rest of the page inert". The Chromium run closed the panel with Escape |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-11 | Closing a modal dialog returns focus to the element focused before it opened, and the `close` event fires after that, so the script moves focus to a passage in its close handler | `notes.js` | ✅ | [HTML, close the dialog](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/interactive-elements.html#close-the-dialog), steps 12 and 13: the focusing steps run for the previously focused element, then the `close` event is queued as a task. The Chromium run's Go to passage left focus on the passage's paragraph, in view |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-12 | The content security policy leaves a `.md` download through a `blob:` link alone, because no directive governs downloads | README | ✅ | [CSP Level 3](https://w3c.github.io/webappsec-csp/): its navigation directives are `form-action` and `frame-ancestors` only. The Chromium run, under the production policy, downloaded `notes-tools.md` with the same text as the export field and logged no violation |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-13 | The policy blocks a style attribute in markup but not a position the script sets through the element's `style` object | `notes.js` | ✅ | The source of row fix/172-glossary-link-every-term-3, above. The Chromium run placed the toolbar with `style.top` and `style.left` and logged no violation |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-14 | `navigator.clipboard.writeText()` works only in a secure context and rejects when writing is not allowed, so the export also fills a field to copy by hand | README, `notes.js` | ✅ | [MDN, `Clipboard.writeText()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Clipboard/writeText): "can only be done in a secure context" and a `NotAllowedError` "if writing to the clipboard is not allowed". The Chromium run read the clipboard back equal to the field; `test/notes.test.ts` checks the field without a clipboard |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-15 | Chrome turns on caret browsing, which lets a reader select text from the keyboard, with F7 | README | ✅ | [Google Accessibility Help, Use a text cursor to navigate and select text](https://support.google.com/accessibility/answer/10129654?hl=en): "This setting is called caret browsing" and "You can also turn on caret browsing with the keyboard shortcut F7". The Chromium run extended a selection with Shift+Arrow; without caret browsing it could not start one from the keyboard |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-16 | Firefox turns on caret browsing with F7 | README | ⚠️ | From a search summary of [Mozilla Support, Accessibility features in Firefox](https://support.mozilla.org/en-US/kb/accessibility-features-firefox): "You can toggle caret browsing off/on by pressing F7". The page did not load for a direct read, and no Firefox run checked it |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-17 | The toolbar and the Notes button move none of the chapter's text, a 390 px screen never scrolls sideways, and printing hides the notes interface | BK24, README | ✅ | The Chromium run: cumulative layout shift on a chapter with a stored highlight was 0.0007 with the script and 0.0007 with it blocked; at 390 px the page's scroll width stayed within its client width with the toolbar and with the panel open; under print media the Notes button's `display` was `none` |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-18 | A highlight whose surrounding text changed is drawn and marked moved, and one the chapter lost is listed as not found with its text and note | BK24, README | ✅ | `test/notes.test.ts`; the Chromium run served the chapter with one sentence reworded and one removed: the first highlight stayed drawn and read "Moved", the second read "Not found" with its note and no Go to passage button, and the export marked it |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-19 | Without JavaScript, without `CSS.highlights` or with storage blocked, the chapter shows no notes interface and logs no error | BK24, README | ✅ | The Chromium run, once with JavaScript off, once with `CSS.highlights` deleted before load and once with the `localStorage` getter throwing: no `[data-notes-ui]` element and no console error in each; `test/notes.test.ts` checks the same in happy-dom |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-20 | Every repeat of a quote is scored together, so a paragraph moved under another heading is found there, marked moved, even when a similar sentence stays in the old section | BK24, README, `notes.js` | ✅ | `test/notes.test.ts`, "a paragraph moved to another section…": the exact context under the new heading wins over the repeat left in the stored section, and the result is "moved"; checked on 2026-11-22 after the first review of PR #247 |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-21 | Each line of the exported quotation is escaped so it reads as plain text: `\`, `` ` ``, `*`, `_`, `[`, `]`, `!`, `&`, `<`, `~` and `\|` everywhere, and `#`, `>`, `+`, `=`, `-` and a number's `.` or `)` at the line's start; a line of only `>` keeps a blank line inside the quotation (corrected by row fix/173-notes-and-highlights-25) | README, BK24, `notes.js` | ✅ | [CommonMark 0.31.2, backslash escapes](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#backslash-escapes): "Any ASCII punctuation character may be backslash-escaped" and "Escaped characters are treated as regular characters"; its block quote section, example 244, separates two paragraphs of one quote with a `>` line. `test/notes.test.ts` parses a hostile export with `remark-parse` 11.0.0 and finds only paragraphs and text in the note's quotation: no heading, list, rule, code, HTML, link, image, definition, emphasis or strong |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-22 | At the 200-highlight cap a passage already highlighted can still take a note, and only a new highlight is refused | README, BK24 | ✅ | `test/notes.test.ts`, "at the cap a passage already highlighted still takes a note…" |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-23 | A refused highlight, whether a repeat of one already saved or a write the browser rejects, keeps the toolbar open and shows why in it | README, BK24 | ✅ | `test/notes.test.ts` and the Chromium run on 2026-11-22: a second Highlight on a saved passage kept the toolbar open with "That passage is already highlighted.", and with `Storage.prototype.setItem` throwing, Highlight kept it open with "This browser would not save the change…" and drew nothing |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-24 | In Firefox 140 to 145 a highlight shows only its background, which is below 3:1 on the page, and a highlight with a note looks like one without | README, BK24 | ✅ | Follows from row fix/173-notes-and-highlights-3 (no `text-decoration` in `::highlight()` before Firefox 146) and row fix/173-notes-and-highlights-6 (1.2:1 light, 1.52:1 dark); the only difference between the two highlight names is the underline's style. Not run in Firefox |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-25 | Row fix/173-notes-and-highlights-21 first said the export escaped only the markers that start a block and `<`; a note could still produce a link, image, reference definition, code span, emphasis or entity | README, BK24, `notes.js` | ✏️ | The medium review of PR #247 (finding 2). `markdownLine()` now also escapes `` ` ``, `*`, `_`, `[`, `]`, `!`, `&`, `~` and `\|` everywhere, row 21 lists the full set, and its test parses inline hostile input as well; the Chromium run exported `\[t\](http://x) \!\[i\](http://x) \`c\` \*e\* \&amp; \<b>x\</b>` |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-26 | Firefox turns on caret browsing with F7, the shortcut on by default | README | ✅ | Firefox's source at commit [`a3178f3`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/a3178f3cbfac6e25d5bc7b6365c4ff520a42205e/toolkit/content/tests/browser/browser_f7_caret_browsing.js): the test `browser_f7_caret_browsing.js` toggles caret browsing by synthesizing `KEY_F7`, and [`modules/libpref/init/all.js`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/a3178f3cbfac6e25d5bc7b6365c4ff520a42205e/modules/libpref/init/all.js) sets `accessibility.browsewithcaret_shortcut.enabled` to `true`. This confirms the claim row fix/173-notes-and-highlights-16 left unverified |
| fix/173-notes-and-highlights-27 | The light accent reaches only 4.02:1 on the light highlight, so highlighted text keeps the text colour and a highlighted link takes it too | README | ✅ | `contrastRatio()` in `src/design/contrast.ts` on the shipped colours: on the light highlight `#F5DF9E`, accent 4.02, link 6.12, soft text 8.14 and muted text 6.04; on the dark `#4F3B1C`, accent 5.01, link 6.6, soft text 6.57 and muted text 4.55. Only the light accent misses 4.5:1, and one `::highlight()` rule colours every element's text, so the rule keeps `color: var(--color-text)` |

| fix/manual-ios-input-zoom-1 | `@media (any-pointer: coarse)` matches when at least one input mechanism is a pointing device of limited accuracy, while `pointer: coarse` tests only the primary one | README, `components.css` | ✅ | MDN, [`any-pointer`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@media/any-pointer): `coarse` is "At least one input mechanism includes a pointing device of limited accuracy", and "More than one value can match if the available devices have different characteristics"; MDN, [`pointer`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@media/pointer): `coarse` is "The primary input mechanism includes a pointing device of limited accuracy, such as a finger on a touchscreen". Read on 2026-11-23 |

Not tested on a real iOS Safari: the Chromium check only measures computed sizes |
| fix/manual-ios-input-zoom-3 | An iPad with a mouse attached reports a fine primary pointer and a coarse `any-pointer`, so a finger can still focus a field there | `components.css` | ⚠️ | Follows from the two MDN definitions in row fix/manual-ios-input-zoom-1 only if the iPad reports the mouse as its primary pointer, which no source read here states and no device was tested |

## Issue #175: offline reading and the book's caching

Claims in [`docs/research/book/offline-reading-and-caching.md`](../../research/book/offline-reading-and-caching.md), checked on 2026-11-22 against Cloudflare's and MDN's pages (each page's own `dateModified` or last-modified date is November 2026 or earlier) and this repository's files. The options table and the recommendation are our own judgement and have no row.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| docs/175-offline-caching-1 | A Worker with static assets sends `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` by default, with an `ETag` that hashes the file | Report, "What a reader gets today" | ✅ | [Cloudflare, static assets headers](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/headers/): "Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate", and the `ETag` "value is a hash of the static asset file" |
| docs/175-offline-caching-2 | Cloudflare caches static assets across its network and serves them without running Worker code when no script is present | Report | ✅ | [Cloudflare, static assets](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/): "Cloudflare automatically caches static assets across its network", and a matching file is served "without invoking Worker code" |
| docs/175-offline-caching-3 | A `Cache-Control` set in `_headers` overrides the default, and two rules that set the same header have their values joined with a comma; `! Cache-Control` detaches an earlier value | Report, option B | ✅ | Same page: "Headers defined in the `_headers` file override what Cloudflare ordinarily sends"; "the values are joined with a comma separator"; the `! Cache-Control` example for one file |
| docs/175-offline-caching-4 | The book's `_headers` gives `/_astro/*` `max-age=31536000, immutable`, and the book is an assets-only Worker | Report | ✅ | `apps/manual/public/_headers` (the `/_astro/*` rule and its comment on content-hashed names); `apps/manual/wrangler.jsonc` ("static assets only, with no Worker code") |
| docs/175-offline-caching-5 | The same `_headers` allows `worker-src 'self'`, which Pagefind needs | Report, option C | ✅ | `apps/manual/public/_headers`, the content security policy and its comment that "worker-src lets it start pagefind-worker.js" |
| docs/175-offline-caching-6 | MDN defines `stale-while-revalidate` as reusing a stale response while it revalidates, and `stale-if-error` as reusing one when an upstream error or a local error occurs | Report, option B | ✅ | [MDN, `Cache-Control`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Cache-Control): "the cache could reuse a stale response while it revalidates it", and "when an upstream server generates an error, or when the error is generated locally" |
| docs/175-offline-caching-7 | Which browsers' HTTP caches honour `stale-while-revalidate` and `stale-if-error` for a page navigation is not established | Report, option B | ⚠️ | MDN's compatibility table did not load in the fetch used for this report, so no browser support was read; the report says a browser test must answer it before the change ships |
| docs/175-offline-caching-8 | Cloudflare's revalidation check "does not keep related requests on one version or prevent version skew" | Report, option C | ✅ | Cloudflare's headers page, the explanation of the default `Cache-Control` |
| docs/175-offline-caching-9 | The plan lists the offline service worker as out of scope, citing the garment book's own build script and offline test | Report, option C | ✅ | `docs/plans/build-book.md` §1 ("Offline reading") and its non-goals table; row 29 of this log |
| docs/175-offline-caching-10 | If the revalidation round trip for a chapter page fails, the page fails to load | Report, "What a reader gets today" | ⚠️ | Our inference: `max-age=0, must-revalidate` is equivalent to `no-cache` per MDN, and a cache that must revalidate cannot reuse a stored copy without the server; no browser test was run, and the report says readers' failure rate is unknown |
| docs/175-offline-caching-11 | Only files under `/_astro/` are hashed and immutable; the fonts (`/fonts/...`), `scripts/theme.js` and the search index (`/pagefind/`) have no hashed names or `Cache-Control` rule, so they keep the default | Report, "What a reader gets today" | ✅ | `apps/manual/src/styles/tokens.css` (`url("/fonts/...")`); `apps/manual/public/scripts/theme.js` and `public/fonts/`; `apps/manual/public/_headers` (the only `Cache-Control` rule is `/_astro/*`); `dist/pagefind/` exists in the built book. The first draft of the report said the fonts and scripts load from cache; the review corrected it, and this row records the corrected claim |
| docs/175-offline-caching-12 | A cache must not serve a stale response when `no-cache` or `must-revalidate` prohibits it, and `must-revalidate` means a stale response is not reused until validated | Report, option B | ✅ | [RFC 9111](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111.html) (June 2022) §4.2.4: "A cache MUST NOT generate a stale response if it is prohibited by an explicit in-protocol directive (e.g., by a no-cache response directive, a must-revalidate response directive, …)"; §5.2.2.2: "a cache MUST NOT reuse that response to satisfy another request until it has been successfully validated by the origin" |
| docs/175-offline-caching-13 | RFC 5861, which defines `stale-while-revalidate` and `stale-if-error`, does not mention `must-revalidate` | Report, option B | ✅ | [RFC 5861](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5861.html) (May 2010, Informational) §3 and §4 read in full; neither names `must-revalidate`. The report's advice to drop it rests on RFC 9111 (row 12), not on RFC 5861 |
| docs/175-offline-caching-14 | The deploy tooling throws unless `_headers` has exactly one `/*` rule | Report, option B | ✅ | `apps/manual/scripts/stage-dist.mjs`, `previewHeaders`: "main's _headers must have exactly one "/*" rule, not N" |

| docs/transcript-recorder-nonzero-exit-2 | A mock run's transcript is written only when its process exited 0 and its output holds a line that starts `ok - `; otherwise the writer prints why and leaves an earlier transcript as it was. | `scripts/transcripts/write.mjs`, `finishedRun()` and `writeTranscript()` | ✅ | The new tests in `scripts/ci/transcripts.test.mjs` run exit 1, exit null and exit 0 with no check, and each leaves the committed file unchanged, byte for byte. |

The Cloudflare runner instead passes `ran` from the worker's result. | Ten runner files | ✅ | `grep -rn "transcript.*write(" verify packages scripts` shows each caller; a caller that omits them throws, as the test for `write()` and `writeTranscript()` shows. |
| docs/transcript-recorder-nonzero-exit-4 | The Copilot runner passes the run's exit code unchanged. | `verify/copilot-sdk/scripts/run-examples.mjs` | ✏️ | I first passed 0 for a block that left its client open.
