## T67a: the manual installs and reads offline

Claims in [ADR 0059](../../decisions/0059-offline-manual-service-worker.md), the manual's README section and the comments of `apps/manual/public/_headers` and `src/offline/`, checked on 2026-11-23 against MDN, web.dev, Chrome's and WebKit's posts, the W3C and WHATWG specifications, Cloudflare's docs and runs on this branch. The design choices are our own and have no row.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T67a-1 | A file `_headers` sets for a path overrides what Cloudflare sends by default, and two rules that set the same header have their values joined | ADR 0059, Headers; `public/_headers` | ✅ | [Cloudflare, static assets headers](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/headers/), read 2026-11-23: "Headers defined in the `_headers` file override what Cloudflare ordinarily sends"; "the values are joined with a comma separator". `/sw.js` and `/offline-manifest.json` set `Cache-Control` in one rule each, so nothing joins |
| T67a-2 | Without a `_headers` rule, a file gets `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so every file but the hashed ones revalidates before reuse | ADR 0059, Context | ✅ | Same page, read 2026-11-23: "Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate"; row docs/175-offline-caching-1 |
| T67a-3 | `manifest-src` falls back to `default-src`, so the book's `default-src 'none'` would block the manifest | ADR 0059, Headers; `_headers` comment | ✅ | [MDN, `manifest-src`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/manifest-src), read 2026-11-23: default-src fallback "Yes. If this directive is absent, the user agent will look for the `default-src` directive." The offline browser check ran with the policy and logged no violation |
| T67a-4 | A worker's default scope is the directory of its script, and only a `Service-Worker-Allowed` header widens it, so a worker at `/sw.js` needs none | ADR 0059, Headers; `_headers` comment | ✅ | [MDN, `ServiceWorkerContainer.register()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/ServiceWorkerContainer/register), read 2026-11-23: "The default `scope` ... is the directory where the service worker script is located", and a broader scope needs the `Service-Worker-Allowed` header |
| T67a-5 | `updateViaCache: "none"` fetches the worker script and its imports from the network on every update check, never from the HTTP cache | `public/scripts/offline.js` | ✅ | Same MDN page: `'none'`: "The HTTP cache will not be used for the main script or its imports." The Service Workers spec's Update algorithm sets the request's cache mode to "no-cache" when the update-via-cache mode is not "all" ([w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#update-algorithm), read 2026-11-23) |
| T67a-6 | A browser installs a new worker only when the script's bytes differ from the installed one, so the build stamps its id and file list into `sw.js` | ADR 0059, Decision; `scripts/offline-build.mjs` | ✅ | [web.dev, The service worker lifecycle](https://web.dev/articles/service-worker-lifecycle), read 2026-11-23: "considered updated if it's byte-different"; Service Workers spec Update algorithm: hasUpdatedResources is true when the stored body "is not byte-for-byte identical with response's body" |
| T67a-7 | A new worker that has installed waits until the old one controls no page, unless it calls `skipWaiting()` | ADR 0059, Updates; README | ✅ | web.dev lifecycle article: "the updated worker will `wait` until the existing worker is controlling zero clients"; "`self.skipWaiting()` prevents the waiting" |
| T67a-8 | If the install promise rejects, the browser discards the new worker, and the old one keeps serving | ADR 0059, Decision | ✅ | web.dev lifecycle article: "If your promise rejects, this signals the install failed, and the browser throws the service worker away." `test/offline-worker.test.ts` checks that install rejects for a shell file of another build |
| T67a-9 | A navigation to a page in the worker's scope makes the browser check `/sw.js` for an update | ADR 0059, Rollback | ✅ | web.dev lifecycle article: an update is triggered by "A navigation to an in-scope page" |
| T67a-10 | Deleting `/sw.js` alone leaves an installed worker in place, because a failed update check keeps the registration | ADR 0059, Rollback; README | ✅ | Service Workers spec, Update algorithm (read 2026-11-23): when the fetched script is null the job is rejected with a TypeError, and only "If newestWorker is null" is the registration removed. A 404 gives a null script through HTML's fetch a classic worker script, which completes with null for a response whose status is not ok |
| T67a-11 | The maskable icon's safe zone is a centred circle with a radius of 40% of the icon's width | `scripts/icons.json`; `test/offline-assets.test.ts` | ✅ | [web.dev, Maskable icons](https://web.dev/articles/maskable-icon): "a circular area in the center of the icon with a radius equal to 40% of the icon width"; [W3C, Web Application Manifest](https://www.w3.org/TR/appmanifest/): the safe zone is a centred circle of radius 40% of the smaller dimension, read 2026-11-23 |
| T67a-12 | A manifest's registered extension is `.webmanifest` and its media type `application/manifest+json` | `_headers` comment; README | ✅ | W3C Web Application Manifest, read 2026-11-23: the IANA-registered extension is ".webmanifest" and servers can send `application/manifest+json` |
| T67a-13 | Chrome's install criteria are HTTPS, `name` or `short_name`, a 192 and a 512 pixel icon, `start_url` and a `display` of `standalone` or similar; Lighthouse dropped its PWA category and Chrome's own installability check replaced it | Todo acceptance; PR description | ✅ | [web.dev, install criteria](https://web.dev/articles/install-criteria) and [Chrome, Revisiting installability criteria](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/update-install-criteria), read 2026-11-23. The browser check's `Page.getInstallabilityErrors` call, Chrome's own check, returned no errors on Chromium build 1243 (Playwright 1.63.0) |
| T67a-14 | `Set-Cookie` is a forbidden response-header name | `src/offline/worker.ts` comment | ✅ | [Fetch Standard](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/), read 2026-11-23: "A forbidden response-header name is a header name that is a byte-case-insensitive match for one of: `Set-Cookie`, `Set-Cookie2`". The worker's check is a second guard for a response that carries one |
| T67a-15 | A navigation's `resultingClientId` names the page it creates, and a subresource request's is empty, so the worker can tie a page's assets to how the page was served | `src/offline/worker.ts`; ADR 0059 | ✅ | [MDN, `FetchEvent.resultingClientId`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/FetchEvent/resultingClientId), read 2026-11-23: "the `id` of the `client` that replaces the previous client during a page navigation"; empty for a subresource request; Baseline widely available since September 2022 |
| T67a-16 | Safari's storage policy covers the Cache API and service worker registrations, and it may evict a site's data after a time without use | ADR 0059, Storage | ✅ | [WebKit, Updates to storage policy](https://webkit.org/blog/14403/updates-to-storage-policy/), read 2026-11-23: the policy covers "localStorage, Cache API, IndexedDB, Service Worker", and eviction can happen "when the site has not been interacted with by the user for some time". The post names no number of days, so the ADR names none |
| T67a-17 | In Chromium, Pagefind's search, its web worker and its WebAssembly included, answers offline from the worker's cache | README; PR description | ✅ | `scripts/offline-browser-check.mjs` on 2026-11-23: an offline search for "sandbox" returned the same 17 links as online. Other engines were not run, so the claim is limited to Chromium |
| T67a-18 | `esbuild` 0.28.2 was already in the lockfile, through `packages/agent-safety`'s devDependencies | ADR 0059, Cost | ✅ | `packages/agent-safety/package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` before this branch |
| T67a-19 | The whole book is about 5 MB: 97 listed files and 4,955,201 bytes in the build of 2026-11-23 | ADR 0059, Decision; README | ✅ | `dist/offline-manifest.json` of `pnpm --filter @buildanagent/manual build` on this branch, 2026-11-23 |
| T67a-20 | Search works offline for any query from the first visit | ADR 0059, README; corrects the first draft of row T67a-17's context | ✏️ | The first build's shell held only Pagefind's component UI, so an offline search worked only for a query already run online; the Opus review found it. The shell now holds Pagefind's module, worker, WebAssembly, entry, meta, index chunks and fragments, and `scripts/offline-browser-check.mjs` on 2026-11-23 ran two queries never run online ("sandbox", 17 links; "gateway", 20 links). With Pagefind left out of the shell, the same check timed out on the first search and failed |
| T67a-21 | The shell is 56 files and 1.62 MB, of which Pagefind is about 1.07 MB, in the build of 2026-11-23 | ADR 0059, README | ✅ | `dist/offline-manifest.json` of `pnpm --filter @buildanagent/manual build` on this branch, 2026-11-23: 1,621,016 bytes in the shell, 1,065,341 of them under `/pagefind/` |
| T67a-22 | Under the default `html_handling`, a request for `/tools` gets a 307 to `/tools/` | ADR 0059; `src/offline/worker.ts` | ✅ | [Cloudflare, HTML handling](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/routing/advanced/html-handling/), read 2026-11-23: the default is `auto-trailing-slash`, under which `/tools` gets a 307 redirect to `/tools/` |
| T67a-23 | Email Address Obfuscation is on by default, replaces addresses with links and adds a decoding script, skips a response marked `Cache-Control: no-transform`, leaves out HTML that Worker code adds, and can be turned off for a path with a Configuration Rule | ADR 0059, "What can rewrite the book on its way"; README | ✅ | [Cloudflare, Email Address Obfuscation](https://developers.cloudflare.com/waf/tools/scrape-shield/email-address-obfuscation/), read 2026-11-23: "Cloudflare enables email address obfuscation automatically when you sign up"; `email-decode.min.js` is injected; it does not apply to "HTML/JavaScript code is specifically added by a [Worker]"; the `no-transform` and Configuration Rule passages |
| T67a-24 | Web Analytics' automatic setup, on by default for a proxied site that uses it, injects a beacon script, and `no-transform` stops the injection | ADR 0059 | ✅ | [Cloudflare, Web Analytics get started](https://developers.cloudflare.com/web-analytics/get-started/), read 2026-11-23: "automatic setup, which is enabled by default"; with `public, no-transform` "the Beacon script will not be automatically injected" |
| T67a-25 | Rocket Loader defers every script and uses non-standard tags | ADR 0059 | ✅ | [Cloudflare, Rocket Loader](https://developers.cloudflare.com/speed/optimization/content/rocket-loader/), read 2026-11-23: it defers "the loading of all of your JavaScript", and "Rocket Loader and Email Obfuscation, use non standard tags" |
| T67a-26 | Auto Minify was deprecated on 2024-08-05 | ADR 0059 | ✅ | [Cloudflare, API deprecations](https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/reference/deprecations/), read 2026-11-23: Auto Minify, deprecation date August 5, 2024, "since the Auto Minify feature was deprecated" |
| T67a-27 | Cloudflare's docs do not say whether these zone features rewrite responses that an assets-only Worker serves | ADR 0059 | ⚠️ | Neither the [static assets page](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/) nor the three feature pages above says so, read 2026-11-23; two community posts found by search disagree (a search summary, not a primary source). The design does not depend on the answer, and `offline:verify` answers it for the live site after the first deploy |
| T67a-28 | The book's chapters hold example addresses such as `bob@example.com`, so obfuscation would change those pages | ADR 0059 | ✅ | `apps/manual/dist/tools/index.html` on 2026-11-23 holds `bob@example.com` in a link and a replay; the browser check's rewriting server changed that page, and the worker saved it and reported it under `rewritten` |
| T67a-29 | After the browser restarts the worker, a page served from the cache still gets only its own build's assets | ADR 0059, README | ✅ | `test/offline-worker.test.ts`, "after a restart, a page served from the cache still gets only this build's assets": a new worker instance over the same caches refuses a newer build's script for that page and serves the matching one |
| T67a-30 | `clients.matchAll({ includeUncontrolled: true, type: "all" })` lists every open page and worker of the origin, controlled or not, so activate can tell whether a page of an older build is still open | `src/offline/sw.ts`; ADR 0059 | ✅ | [MDN, `Clients.matchAll()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Clients/matchAll), read 2026-11-23: `includeUncontrolled` "return all service worker clients who share the same origin", default `false`; `type` `"all"` matches every client type, default `"window"` |

## fix/manual-note-editor-flow: the note field takes focus on open and the editor closes on Save and Remove

| # | Claim | Where it appears | Verdict | Source and reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fix/manual-note-editor-flow-1 | iOS Safari opens the on-screen keyboard for a programmatic `focus()` only when the call runs inside the user's tap handler, not after a timer, an `await` or a transition | README, `notes.js` comments | ⚠️ | No Apple or WebKit documentation read states the rule. Secondary sources agree: [Adam Bradley's keyboard-focus test page](https://adamdbradley.github.io/mobile-ux-issues/keyboard-focus.html) (a `setTimeout` focus does not open the keyboard, one inside the touch event's call stack does) and [WebKit bug 195884](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=195884), whose discussion treats the gesture requirement as deliberate. Found by search on 2026-11-23 and read as summaries, not at a shipped iOS version. Not tested on a real iPhone: Playwright cannot show the keyboard |
| fix/manual-note-editor-flow-2 | In Playwright's WebKit build 26.6, a touch tap on an element whose `pointerdown` handler calls `preventDefault()` fires no `click`, while the same tap on an element without that handler does | README, `notes.js` comment | ✅ | Measured on 2026-11-23: `tap()` on a toolbar swatch logged pointerdown, touchstart, pointerup and touchend but no click; the same tap on the Notes button logged a click; after the toolbar's handler skipped touch pointers the swatch tap logged its click. Playwright's WebKit, not a device, so whether iOS Safari does the same is unverified |
| fix/manual-note-editor-flow-3 | The Chromium and WebKit checks prove the field has focus right after the tap that opens the editor, and that the editor closes and leaves focus outside itself; they cannot show the iOS keyboard opening | README | ✅ | `scripts/note-editor-flow-check.mjs` run on 2026-11-23: all 72 checks in desktop and iPhone 14 Chromium passed on the review run, and the iPhone 14 WebKit part, which adds checks, was skipped there for want of a WebKit build. The script reads `document.activeElement` only |

## T67b: the reader saves the whole manual for offline reading

Claims in the save control's reader-facing copy (`apps/manual/public/scripts/save-offline.js`), the ADR 0059 addendum, the manual's README section and the comments of `src/offline/`, checked on 2026-11-23 against MDN, WebKit's posts, the WHATWG Fetch Standard and runs on this branch. The design choices are our own and have no row.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T67b-1 | `navigator.storage.estimate()` reports the site's `usage` and `quota` in bytes, both approximate, and rejects with a `TypeError` when the user has turned storage off | `save-offline.js` (the room shown); README | ✅ | [MDN, `StorageManager.estimate()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/StorageManager/estimate), read 2026-11-23: `quota` is "a conservative approximation", and "The returned values are not exact"; it rejects with a `TypeError` "when the user has disabled storage". The control therefore says "about" and treats a rejection as no estimate |
| T67b-2 | A write past an origin's quota through the Cache API fails with a `QuotaExceededError` | ADR 0059 addendum; `src/offline/worker.ts` | ✅ | [MDN, Storage quotas and eviction criteria](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria), read 2026-11-23: "Attempting to store more than an origin's quota using IndexedDB, Cache, or OPFS, for example, fails with a `QuotaExceededError` exception"; [WebKit, Updates to storage policy](https://webkit.org/blog/14403/updates-to-storage-policy/): "a QuotaExceededError exception will be thrown". `test/offline-save.test.ts` and `save-offline-check.mjs` throw that error from `Cache.put` |
| T67b-3 | A browser may clear a site's saved data when the device needs space | Control copy ("Your browser may still clear it when the device needs space"); ADR 0059 | ✅ | MDN, same page: best-effort data persists "as long as the origin is below its quota, the device has enough storage space"; WebKit, same post: origins are deleted under a "least-recently-used policy" |
| T67b-4 | Safari may clear the saved copy after seven days of Safari use in which the reader does not open the manual | Control copy ("Safari may clear it if you use Safari for a week without opening the manual"); ADR 0059 addendum | ✅ | [WebKit, Full Third-Party Cookie Blocking and More](https://webkit.org/blog/10218/full-third-party-cookie-blocking-and-more/), read 2026-11-23: script-writable storage, "Service Worker registrations and cache" included, is deleted "after seven days of Safari use without user interaction on the site"; MDN, same page as T67b-2: "If an origin has no user interaction, such as click or tap, in the last seven days of browser use, its data created from script will be deleted", when cross-site tracking prevention is on. The copy says "may" because the rule applies only with that prevention on, and a Home Screen web app keeps its own count of days |
| T67b-5 | Firefox answers `navigator.storage.persist()` with a permission prompt, while Safari and Chromium-based browsers decide without one; WebKit grants it on heuristics such as being opened as a Home Screen web app | ADR 0059 addendum; README (why the control does not call it) | ✅ | MDN, same page as T67b-2, read 2026-11-23: in Firefox "the user is notified with a UI popup"; "Safari and most Chromium-based browsers ... automatically approve or deny the request ... and do not show any prompts"; WebKit, Updates to storage policy: "WebKit currently grants a request based on heuristics like whether the website is opened as a Home Screen Web App" |
| T67b-6 | A message a page posts to its service worker arrives with `source` set to the sending `Client`, whose `frameType` is `top-level`, `auxiliary`, `nested` or `none`; `Client.frameType` is Baseline widely available | `src/offline/worker.ts` (`fromBookWindow`); ADR 0059 | ✅ | [MDN, `ExtendableMessageEvent.source`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/ExtendableMessageEvent/source): "a reference to the `Client` object from which the message was sent"; [MDN, `Client.frameType`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Client/frameType): one of `"auxiliary"`, `"top-level"`, `"nested"` or `"none"`, "available across browsers since April 2018"; read 2026-11-23. `save-offline-check.mjs` saved, cancelled and removed through it in Chromium, so a real page's tab passes the check |
| T67b-7 | The book's content security policy already forbids every site from framing it | ADR 0059 | ✅ | `apps/manual/public/_headers` on this branch: `frame-ancestors 'none'` |
| T67b-8 | A response the worker builds with the `Response` constructor has the type `default`, so the store rules that require `basic` refused a page an older worker had stamped | ADR 0059 addendum; `src/offline/worker.ts` | ✅ | [WHATWG Fetch Standard](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/), read 2026-11-23: a response's type is "basic", "cors", "default", "error", "opaque" or "opaqueredirect", and "Unless stated otherwise, it is \"default\"". `test/offline-save.test.ts` carries a saved page through two updates, which failed before the fix |
| T67b-9 | The progress bar's width, set through the CSS object model, breaks no rule of the book's policy, whose `style-src 'self'` allows no inline style | `save-offline.js` | ✅ | `scripts/save-offline-check.mjs` on 2026-11-23 logged no content security policy violation in Chromium (Playwright's build) while the bar moved from 0 to 100%. Other engines were not run, so the claim is limited to Chromium |
| T67b-10 | The whole book was 98 files and 5,008,000 bytes, about 5 MB, with a shell of 57 files, in this branch's build of 2026-11-23 | README; PR description | ✅ | `dist/offline-manifest.json` of `pnpm --filter @buildanagent/manual build` on this branch, 2026-11-23; the shell gained `/scripts/save-offline.js` |
| T67b-11 | A saved manual opens offline in Chromium: a random chapter, the glossary and a search never run before, and the control shows the saved date and build | Todo acceptance; PR description | ✅ | `scripts/save-offline-check.mjs` on 2026-11-23: 24 checks passed, including the progress bar reaching 100%, offline pages `/stopping-and-structured-output/` and `/glossary/`, a search for "approval", removal, a refused quota and the 390 pixel iPhone 14 context with 44 pixel buttons |
| T67b-12 | After the first review's fixes the whole book was 98 files and 5,011,394 bytes, with a shell of 57 files and 1,648,200 bytes, of which Pagefind was 1,065,359; the README and ADR 0059 say 57 files and 1.65 MB | README; ADR 0059, The shell on install; corrects row T67b-10's byte count and the README's stale 56 files and 1.62 MB | ✏️ | `dist/offline-manifest.json` of `pnpm --filter @buildanagent/manual build` on this branch after the review fixes, 2026-11-23. The README and the ADR still gave T67a's shell of 56 files and 1.62 MB, which the Opus review found; the control's script is the 57th file |
| T67b-13 | `self.serviceWorker`, the worker's own `ServiceWorker` object, is missing in some widely used browsers, so the worker falls back to the registration's state and then to its pending record | `src/offline/sw.ts`; ADR 0059 addendum | ✅ | [MDN, `ServiceWorkerGlobalScope.serviceWorker`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/ServiceWorkerGlobalScope/serviceWorker), read 2026-11-23: "Limited availability ... does not work in some of the most widely-used browsers" |
| T67b-14 | After `caches.delete(name)`, opening that name again creates a new, empty cache, while a `Cache` object taken before the delete goes on naming the deleted one, so a save must open the cache by name to see that it is gone | `src/offline/worker.ts` (`#stillSaving`); ADR 0059 addendum | ⚠️ | The Service Workers specification keeps a name-to-cache map that `delete` changes and `open` fills with a new cache for a missing name (my reading of [w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/), 2026-11-23). No browser test on this branch deletes a cache during a save, so the claim rests on the spec and the fake, which models it this way |
| T67b-15 | A save whose worker stops answering ends as partial once STATUS reports no save running, and a Remove the worker never answers gives up after its timeout | ADR 0059 addendum; README | ✅ | `test/save-offline.test.ts` on 2026-11-23, "a save whose worker stops answering" and "a Remove the worker never answers"; each test fails when the watchdog or the timeout is taken out |
| T67b-16 | After Remove, neither a build that was waiting nor one installed later offers the removed copy back: the active worker marks every other build's cache, install carries nothing over from a marked cache, and a build whose own cache is marked keeps only its shell when it activates | ADR 0059 addendum; README | ✅ | `test/offline-save.test.ts` on 2026-11-23: "Remove while a new build waits" and "Remove marks an older cache an open tab still reads". Each fails when the mark, the purge at activate or the carry-over skip is taken out. The medium review found the gap |
| T67b-17 | A cancel that comes while the last file is in flight ends the save as saved, and a full cache whose closing record was never written reads as saved | ADR 0059 addendum; corrects the addendum's earlier rule that STATUS reports `saved` only for a complete record | ✏️ | `test/offline-save.test.ts` on 2026-11-23, "a Cancel that lands while the last file is in flight" and "a full cache whose closing record was never written"; both fail with the earlier rule, which showed "Partly saved: 4 of 4 files" |
| T67b-18 | A partial save names `storage` as its reason when the browser refused a write for a reason other than quota | `src/offline/worker.ts` (`SaveDone`); ADR 0059 protocol table | ✏️ | The TSDoc and the table said so, but the worker set a reason only for quota, which the medium review found. `test/offline-save.test.ts`, "another refused write", now checks the reason and fails without the fix |
| T67b-19 | A copy the reader saves again after Remove, while a new build waits, survives that build's activation and is offered as the copy to update | ADR 0059 addendum; README | ✅ | `test/offline-save.test.ts` on 2026-11-23, "a save made after Remove, while a new build waits, survives that build's activation"; it fails when the activation ignores the mark's time. The low review found the gap |

## T68a: research papers in the manual are named with a link and a description

The two blurbs the register's worked examples carry, checked on 2026-11-23 against each paper's arXiv abstract page and its full PDF (title page, abstract, Section 1 and Section 2). The other papers stay on `apps/manual/src/data/papers-pending.json` until the content todos write and check their blurbs.

| # | Claim | Where | Verdict | Source and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T68a-1 | SkillCFI is a November 2026 arXiv paper by Ruan et al. on control-flow integrity for agent skills | `apps/manual/src/data/sources.json` (`ruan-skillcfi` blurb); shown in Chapters 3 and 13 | ✅ | [arXiv:2611.24185](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24185), read 2026-11-23: v1 submitted 18 November 2026; authors Bonan Ruan, Yuhao Sun, Jiahao Liu, Qixiao Lin, Jian Mao and Zhenkai Liang; title "… Control-Flow Integrity for Agent Skills". Section 1 of the PDF: "We present SkillCFI, which carries CFI over to skill-based agents" |
| T68a-2 | Voyager is a May 2023 arXiv paper by Wang et al. on an agent that plays Minecraft through GPT-4 | `apps/manual/src/data/sources.json` (`wang-voyager` blurb); shown in Chapters 12 and 13 | ✅ | [arXiv:2305.16291](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16291), read 2026-11-23: v1 submitted 25 May 2023 (the register pins v2, 19 October 2023); first author Guanzhi Wang. Abstract: "the first LLM-powered embodied lifelong learning agent in Minecraft"; Section 1: Voyager works "with a blackbox LLM (GPT-4) through prompting and in-context learning"; Section 2 (Method) has GPT-4 propose tasks and write each skill's program |

**Totals:** 799 claims: 689 ✅, 98 ✏️, 12 ⚠️.
